और नक्त पाइप लाइन बिछाने के प्रयोजन के 
लिए अधि चना के संलग्न अनुसूची में वणित भमि में 
उपयोग के अधिार का अर्जन करना आवश्यक है । 

अत अब , केन्द्रीय सरकार, पट्रोलियम और खनिज 
पाइप लाइन ( भूमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन ) 
अधिनियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 3 की 
उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते 
हए उनमें उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन करने के 
अपने प्राशय की घोषणा करती है । 

उका अनुसूची में वणित भूमि में हितबद्ध कोई 
व्यक्ति उस तारीख से जिसको, राजपत्र में यथा प्रकाशित 
इस अधिसूचना की प्रतियां साधारण जनता को उपलब्ध 
करा दी जाती हैं , 21 दिन के भीतर भूमि के नीचे 
पाइप लाइन बिछाने के संबंध में उन में उपयोग के अधि 
अधिकार का अर्जन करने संबंधी लिखित रूप में प्राक्षेप 
श्री बी . एन , अखौरी , सक्षम प्राधिकारी , इंडियन प्रायल 
कारपोरेशन लिमिटेड, हल्दिया यशैनी ऋड पाइपलाइन 
परियोजना, कलेज रोड, विलियम्म टाउन , देवघर - 814112 
( बिहार ) को कर सकेगा ।

S .O 981 . Whercas, it appears to the Central Govers 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of petroleum ( crude ) from Haldia in the state of 
West Bengal to Barauni in the state of Bihar, pipeline should 
be laid by the Indian Oil Corporation Limited ; 

And whereas it appcars that for the purpose of laying 
such pipelincs it is necessary to acquire the right of usor in 
the land described in the schedule annexed to this notification ; 

Now , therefore, in the exercise of the powers conferred 
by sub -section (1 ) of section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 
Pipelines ( Acquisition of the Right of User in Land ) Act , 
1962 ( 50 of 1962) , the Central Government hereby declares 
its intention to acquire the right of user therein ; 

Any person interested in the land described in the said 
schedule may within 21 days from the date on which the 
copies of this notification , as published in the Gazette of 
India , are made available to general public, objection in 
writing to the acquisition of the right of user therein or 
laying of the pipeline under the land to Shri V . N . Akhoury , 
competent authority , Indian Oil Corporation Limited , 
Haldia -Barauni Crude Pipeline Project, College Road, Wil 
liams Town, Deoghar- 814112 (Bihar ).

का . आ . 2986 :- केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता 
है कि लोक हित में ऐसा करना आवश्यक है कि पश्चिमी

लियम ( ऋड ) के परिवहन के लिए इंडियन प्रॉयल कारपो 
रेशन लि . द्वारा पाइपलाईन बिछाई जाए ; 


[ सं . प्रार- 31015/ 11/ 96- ओ . प्रार . - --1] 

के . सी , कटोच , अवर सचिव 


और उक्त पाइपलाइन बिछाने के प्रयोजन के लिए 
अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनु सूची में वर्णित भूमि में उपयोग के 
अधिकार का प्रर्जन करना आवश्यक है ; 


New Delhi, the 14th October, 1996 


S . O . - - 2985 — Whercas , it appears to the Central Govern . 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of petroleum ( crude) . from - Haldia in the state of 
West Bengal to Barauni in the state of Bihar, pipeline should 
be laid by the Indian Oil Corporation Limited ; 


अतः, अब, केन्द्रीय सरकार , पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाईप 
लाईन ( भूमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन ) अधिनियम , 
1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 3 की उपधारा ( 1 ) 
धारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए उनमें उपयोग के 
अधिकार का अर्जन करने के अपने प्राशय की घोषणा करती 


Agd , whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline it is nccessary to acquire the right of user in 
the land described in the Schedule annexed to this notification ; 


Now , therefore, in extrcise of the powers conferred 
by sub -section (1 ) of section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 
Pipehines (Acquisition of the right of User in Land ) Act, 
1962 ( 50 of 1962 ), the Central Government hereby declares 
its intention to acquire tho right of user tharcin ; 


Any person interested in land described in the said schedule 
may within 21 days from the date on which the copies of this 
notification , as published in the Gazette of India , are made 
available to general public , objóction in writing to the ac 
quisition of the right of user thercio or laying of the pipe 
line under the land to Shri Biswanath Bose , Competent 
Authority , Indian Oil Corporation Limited , Haldia - Barauni 
Crude Pipolinc Project, P . O . Haldia Rofinbry, Distt . Midna 
pur , West Bengal. 


उन्त अनुसूची में वर्णित भूमि में हितकर कोई व्यक्ति 
उस सारीख से जिसको, राजपत्र में यथा प्रकाशित इस अधि 
सूचना की प्रतियां साधारण जनता को उपलब्ध करा दी जाती 
है , 21 दिन के भीतर भूमि के नीचे पाइप लाइन बिछाने 
के संबंध में उनमें उपयोग के अधिकार का प्रभाकरने संबंधी 
लिखित रूप में प्रक्षेप. श्री विश्वनाथ बोस, सक्षम प्राधिकारी 
इंडियन- अभयल कारपोरेशन लिमिटेड, हल्दिया, बीनी 
पाईपलाईन परियोजना, डाक घर हल्दिया रिफाइमरी जिला 
मिदनापुर , पश्चिमी बंगाल को कर सकेगा ।

transport of petroleum (crude ) from Haldia in the State of 
West Bengal to Barauni in the State of Bihar, pipeline should 
be laid by the Indian Oil Corporation Limited ; 


67 


56 


And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying such 
pipeline it is necessary to acquire the right of user in the 
land described in the schedule annexed to this potification ; 


Now , therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub -section (1) of section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 
Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in Land ) Act, 1962 
( 50 of 1962 ), the Central Government hereby declares its 
intention to acquire the right of user therein ; 


Any person interested in the land described in the said 
schedulo may within 21 days from the date on which the 
copies of this notification , as published in the Gazetto of 
India , are made available to general public , objection in wri 
ting to the acquisition of the right of user theroin or laying 
of the pipeline under the land to Shri Biswanath Boso, Compe 
tent Authority , Indian Oil Corporation Limited , Haldia 
Barauni Pipeline Project, P . O . Haldia Refinery , Distt . 
Midnapur, West Bengal .

S .O . 2988 . - Whereas, it appears to thic Central Gover 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of petroleum (crudc) froin Haldia in the state of 
West Bengal to Barauni in the state of Pihar , pipeline should 
be laid by the Indian Oil Corporation Limited ; 

And , whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying such 
pipeline , it is nocessary to acquire the right of user in th , 
land described in the Scheduled annexed to this notification ; 

Now , therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub - section ( 1) of section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 
Pipelinos (Acquisition of Right of User in Land ) Act , 1962 
( 50 of 1962), tho Central Government hereby declares its 
intention to require the right of user therein ; 

Any person interested in the land described in the said 
schedulo may within 21 days from the date on which the 
copies of this not fication , as published in the Gazetro of 
India , are made available to general public , objection in 
writing to the acquisition of the right of user thercin for laya 
ing of the pipeling under the land to Shri Biswanath Rose ,

Act, 1947 for adjudication of the industrial dis

follows : 


tial part of the sovereign functions of 
the State as a Welafre State . It is not, 
therefore , an industry . 


In view of the specific findings of the Supreme 
Court, Telecom Department is not an industry . 
This Tribunal has no jurisdiction to adjudicato 
lipon the dispute between the Government Depart 
ment and its employee, 


" Whether the action of the management of 

Telecom Distt . Manager Ananthapur is 
justified in terminating the service of Sri

This reference has been registered as Industrial 
Dispute No. 85 of 1995 on the file of this Tribu 
nal. After notice was issued by this Tribunal, to 
both the parties , the workman filed claim state 
ment and the Respondent filed a counter . 


2 . The learned Advocate for the Respondent 
Management requested this Tribunal to decide the 
legality of the reference as a preliminary point, in 
view of the fact that the Supreme Court held that 
the Telecom Department is not an industry within 
the meaning of Section 2 (i ) of the Industrial Dis 
putes Act, 1947 . 


6 . However the learned Advocate for the Peti 
tioner workman raised several contentions . He 
aruged that the Supreme Court did not refer to the 
two leading decisions on the subject viz . Bangalore 
Water Supply & Sewerage Board v . A , Rajappa 
(AIR 1978 S . C ., Page 969 - - 1978 ( I ) LLJ Page 
349 and also Chief Conservative of Forests V . 
Jagannath Maruti Kondhare and Others ( 1996 
S . C . C . ( I. & S ) 500 ) which dealt with the sovereign 
functions of the State . He also referred to N . Nag 
endra Rao & Co. v . State of Andhra Pradesh ( 1994 
6 S . C ., Page 205 ) wherein the sovereign functions 
of the State are set out. He relied upon the deci . 
sion in Delhi Scicncc Ferum v . Union of India 
and another ( AIR , 1996 S . C . , Page 1356 ) which 
upheld the new Telecom policy of the Government 
by which a part of tlie industry is entrusted to pri 
vate enterprises . He lastly argued at length and 
referred to several decisons referred to page 477 
of The A . I. R . Manual ( Vth Edition ) wherein the 
word " might" used in Section 11 of C . P . C . was 
considered . He argued that the Supreme Court 
might have or ought to have considered several 
other decisions on the subject and they were not 
considered 


3 . Both the parties are heard on the point. 


4 . The point for consideration is whether this 
Trihunal has got jurisdiction to adjudicate the dis 
pute ? 


5 . POINT - The Petitioner- workman was em 
ployed in the Telecom Department. The Govern 
ment of India made a reference to this Tribunal 
treating the Telecom Department as an industry 
and the Petitioner as a workman coming with n the 
purview of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. The 
Supreme Court in Sub -Divisional Inspector of Post , 
Vaikam and Others v . Thevvan Joseph etc , ( 1996 
LLR Page 483 ), While deciding the case of Postal 
Department which is a part of Telecom Depart 
ment held as follows : 


7 . This Tribunal as a subordinate to High Court 
and Supreme Court cannot distinguish any deci 
sions given by Supreme Court directly on a parti 
cular point by referring to the decisions of the 
Supreme Court or of our High Court on a sinilar 
point. The Supreme Court held that the Telecom 
Department is not an industry . When once the em 
ployer is not an industry , there cannot be an in 
dustrial dispute and so this Tribunal cannot enter 
tain the dispute , if any, between the petitioner and 
the Respondent, ), therefore, hold that this Tribu 
nal has no jurisdiction to entertain this dispute . 


8 . In the result , an Award is passed according 


ly . 


" Having regard to the contentions, the ques 

tion arices whether the anpallant is an 
industry ? India as a sovereign socialist , 
secular democratic republic has to estab 
Jish an egalitarian social order under 
rule of law . The welfare measures part 
take the character of Sovereign func 
tions and the traditional duty to main 
tuin law and order is no longer the 
concept of Siate . Directive principles of 
State policy enjoin on the State diverse 
duties under Part IV of the Constitution 
and the performance of the duties are 
constitutional functions . One of the 
cstyl is of the State to provide telecom 
munication service to the general pub

( PART - SEC . 3 ( ii ) 


mm 


been referred to an Industrial Tribunal for adjudi 
cation . In the course of such adjudication , the Tri 
bunal has to be satisfied that the order of discharge 
or dismissal was not justified . If it comes to such 
{ l conclusion , the Tribupal has to set aside the order 
and direct reinstatement of the workman on such 
terms as it think fit . The Tribunal has also power 
to give any other relief to the workman including 
the imposing of a lesser punishment having due 
regard to the circumstances ." 


the reason s .ated theicin was bis “ personal work " . On 4 - 3 -85 
he had sent another lev , letter Ex . M .ll seeking one day 
casual cave to elejid sonne domestic work . I here 2150 he has 
not menuonea his uncss . On 5 - 3 - 85 he had plated that

lt jy oniy oiler 5 - -85 , he had sent Ex . M - 13 for cancellation 
of 11e casual Jeuvo be ween 1 - 5 -85 and 5 - 3 - 85 god to commute 
the same as Sick Leave till 9 - 3 -85 . The Branch Manager 
has found out the contrad .ctory reasony eta . ed by him in 
his leave application , and directed hun to obtain Medical 
Certificate from the District Medicul Ollicer . It is clear 
from he evidence of Thangavelų , Peon of that bank that 
the worknan was not in nis house on 1 - 3 - 85 at about 
10 . 30 am . When Palanisamy one of the Clerks went to he 
residence and intormed him of the shortage of Rs. 200 he 
said that he was unable to come to the branch on that day 
he was liwell. frowever , if he was unwell on 1 - 3 - 85 , hc 
could have stated the said reason in the casual leava appli 
Cation EX M . 10 whercas he had stated that due 10 some 
important personal work he could not attend olicc on 1 - 3 - 85 
and 2 - 3 -85. Therefore , the rcagons stated by the workman 
for his continuous absence from 1 - 3 -85 was contradictory and 
the same ÇADnot be accepted . The workman never denied 
the shortage und he has paid Rs. 200 us directed by the 
Branch Munager, He has explained it by saying that in order 
to fulfil the duections of the Branch Manager, he had paid 
the amount and the same should be returned to him atter 
enquiry . It is nothing but an explanation in order to show 
that he was inuent, of the charge . I he behaviour of the 
workman leaving the bank at alyut 9 .30 2 . m . and applying 
for leave since then and extending the leave under some 
pretext or other would go to show live hollowness in his plen 
of innocence . A perusal of the findings given by the Enquiry 
Officer would go to show that he has assessed cvery one 
of the reasons for and against the workman and finally had 
come to the conclusion that the charge has been proved 
Against the workman . The findings cannot be called as unsatis 
factory much less perverse . 


The earlier decision in Chartered Bank s case shows that this 
Tribunal can go beyond the form and look at the substance 
and if it comes to tho conclusion that though in forni the 
order amounts to termination simpliciter , it in reality cloaks 
o dismissal for the misconduct. It will be open to set it aside 
as a colourable excrcise of the power . That was the position 
prior to the introduction of $ . 11 - A as on 15th December , 
1971, After the introduction of S . 11A , if the Tribunal in 
satisfied that the order of discharge or dismissal was not 
justified , it can set aside the order and direct the reinstatc . 
ment of the workman und the Tribunal has power to give 
any other relicf to the workman including imposing of lessel 
punishment having due regard to the circumstances. There 
fore, Section 11 - A gives power to the Tribunal to impose 
a just punishment . When we consider the fucts of the instant 
cuse in the light of the above ruling, wo cannot disagree 
with the finding of the Enguiry Offlcer . Once this Tribunul 
has come to the conclusion of confirming the view taken by 
the Enquiry Officer , it was arglice that the Tribunal has no 
power to interfere with the 19nishment Awarded by the 
Management . However , Section 11 - A of the Industrial Dis 
putcs Act gives imple power to the Tribunal to give any 
other relief to the workman including imposing of lesser 
punishment having " due regard to the circumstances . I 
the instant case both the churges framed against the work 
man have been proved . Charge No. 2 which deals with ic . 
moving 4 currency noles of Rs. 50 denomination each is a 
serious one, considering the nature of work of the workman , 
The workman who is working in the bank has removed the 
currency notes kept under his safe custody . There cannot 
be any other serious violation of his official dutics than this . 
The employees of the Bank who deal in cash arc real cus 
todians of the public money . If any Bank employee has in 
dulged in the misconduct of removing the currency notes 
kept in the locker no other punishment except dismissal can 
be awarded to such person . Under these circumstances , the 
punishment awarded to the workman by the management is 
just and proper and the same has to be confirmed . 


6 . It was argued on the side of the workınan that the 
Enquiry Officer took tbe role of the management represen 
tative in the departmental enquiry and put questions to MW2 
in the re - examination . The enquiry proceedingy contain the 
questions put by the Enquiry Officer and also the answers 
given by MW2. The Enquiry Oficer has got right to clarify 
the doubts arise in the ambiguous sta ’ ements given by the 
witacys. Accordingly , tho Enquily Officer hng elicited certain 
facts from the mouth of MW2 and the same cannot be 
shown against the Enquiry Officer s partisan attitude towards 
the workman . From the evidence available on the l ecord , 
it would lead to the irresistable conclusion that charge No. 2 
has been proved against the workman . 


lu the result, an award is passed rejecting the claim of the 
petitioner - No costs. 
Dated , this the 7th day of August , 1996 . 

S . THANGARAJ, Industrial Tribunal 


7 . The workman has got challenged the departmental en 
quiry and the findings of the Enquiry Oficer and asked for 
preliminary enquiry before this Tribuna ), The petitioner 
union hus argued its case under Section 11- 4 of the I. D . 
Act, Section 11- A was introduced in the I. D . Act on 15th 
December, 1971. In order to show the position of law be 
fore 15th December, 1971 and the legal positiion us on 15th 
December , 1971 two rulings of the Supreme Court were cited 
before me. In Chartered Bank , Bombay vs . Chartered Bank 
Employees Union & Another ( 1960 II LLJ P . 222 ) the 
Supreme Court has held : 


WITNESSES EXAMINED

" The form of the order of termination is not conclu 

sive of the true nature of the order, for it is possi 
ble that the form may be merely a camouflage for 
An order of dismissal for misconduct. It is thereforc , 
always open to the Tribunal to go behind the form 
and look at the substance ; and if it comes to the 
conclusion , for example, that though in form the 
order amounts to termination simpliciter, it in rea 
lity cloaks a dismissal for misconduct , it will be 
open to set it aside as a colourable exercise of the 
power . "

S . O . 2998.- -- Whereas the Central Government is satisfied 
that the public interest requires that the services in the indus 
try engaged in the manufacture or production of minoral oil 
( Crude oil ) , motor and avintion spirit , diesel oil , Kerosene 
oil , fuel oil, diverse hydrocarbon oils and their blends in 
cluding synthetic fuels , lubricating oils and the like, which 
are covered by entry 26 in the First Schedule to the Indus 
irial Disputes Act, 1947 ( 14 of 1947 ) , should be declarcal 
to be a public utility sorvice for the purposes of the said 
Act . 

Now , therefore , in cxercise of the powers conferred by sub 
section ( vi ) of clause ( n ) of Section 2 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947 ) , the Contral Government 
hereby declares with immediate effect the said Industry to 
ho a public utility servico for the purpose of the said Act for 
a period of six months.

7 . At the outset it may be mentioned that the award of 
P . C . Sharma has not been filed . Hence it cannot be ascer 
tained whether this Tribunal had given any award under 
the facts and circumstances as they exist in the instant case . 
In the absence of award the contention of the opposte party 
in this regard is repelled , 

8 . In the second place defence of settlement may be taken 
up . The settlcments are alleged to have taken place on 
17 - 11 -87, 27 - 10 - 88 and in January , 1991 that is during the 
pendency of instant reference . In the case of Mis Hari 
Fertilizers Sahupuri versus State of U . P . 1991 Lah IC 777 
it has been held that when any settlemcnt is arrived at 
during the existence of reference and the workman is not a 
party to it then such settlement will have no binding effect 
on tlic workman . In vicw of this authority I arrive at the 
conclusion that the above mentioned settlements will not 
be any impediment to the getting of any relief in the instant 
case .

against both the employees . 


loyces were beard by Metropolitan Magistratc, Hom

The Govt. of India , Ministry ofLabour, in exercise 
of the power conferred on them under Section 10 1 ) 
( d ) of the I . D . Act 1947 has referred the following 
dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication vide their 
Orders referred to above .

1993

" The truc test may be indicated once again , 

where a worker or group of workers labours 
to produce goods or services and these 
goods or services are l or the business of un 
other, that other is, in fact , the employer. He 
has economic control over the workers sub 
sistence skill, and continued employment, 11 
he , for any reasons, chokes off , the worker is 
virtually laid - oll . The presence of intennedi 
ate contractors with whom alone the workers 
have in mcdiate or direct relationship ex 
contract is of no consequence when , on 
lifting the veil or looking at the conspectus 
of factors governing employment, WC dis 
cover the naked truth , though draped in 
different paper arriageinent, tha : the real 
employer is the management, not the imme 
Jiate contractor. N ? yriad devices, balf-hid 
deu in fold after fold of legal form depending 
on the degree of concealmout necdou , 1ype 
of industry the local conditions and the 
like may be resorted to when labour legis 
latio . custs welfares obligations on the real 
< inployer, based on Arts . 38 , 39 , 42 , 43 
and 43A of the Constitution . The Court 
must be astate to avoid the mischief and 
achieve the purpose of the law and not be 

misled by the may of legal appearances " , 
and also my attention was drawn to the latest case of 
Gujarat Elecricity Board Thermal Power Station 
versus- Hind Mazdoor Sabha reported in 1995 Lab 
1 . C . puge 2807 whçic the scope and nature of Section 
10 of 1. D . Act with reference to Contract Labour 
( Regulation and Abolition ) Act, 1970 have been con 
sidered and also in this case what will be the position 
of the workmen working under the contractor has 
also been discussed and decided . 

18 . Ther Lordships ju the said case law have 
clcarly stated that the inam question which is to be 
considered by the Tribunal whether the existence of 
the contractor is a camouflage and sham in order to 
defeat the right of the workpien . It is also decided 
that in that case the Tribunal has the power to decide 
the dispute under Section 10 . Their Lordships have 
also given guitlelines with obscrvation that in a pro 
hibited category of job no workinan can be appointed 
through contractor and that is against law and if the 
Tribunal is of the opinion that the existence of the 
contracta : or contract is sham or bogus or camou 
flagc to conceal the truth then the said contractor 
would be considered as merely intermediaries and the 
real employer would be the management for whom 
the workmen have worked . 


23 , It is needless to say that the job performed by 
thu is admittedly permanent lid perennial nature and 
thereby jrrespective of their continuance in job they 
should be considered to be the workmen according 
to the clefinition of the workman even under the 
I. D . Act and this gives the jurisdiction of this Tri 
bunal to decide the question under Section 10 with 
reference to Section 257 of the I . D . Act. 


24 . Therefore , in unung lip the aforesaid dis 
cussions I have no hesitation iv hold that the con 
cemed workmen of both references are the workmen 
of the management of B . C . C .L . as they havc peT 
formed ihe job in perinament and perennial in nature 
beyond the statutory period as required and in vicw 
of the present trend of the decisions they should be 
considered as the workers of the management and not 
of the contractor and this Court has jurisdiction to 
decide that question anal they should be regularised 
in the manner as stated below as they have performed 
the job of permanent incl perennial nature in the 
mutner as discussed above . 


25 . As they were not under the employment of the 
management in regular manner but they have acquired 
right to be regularised in view of the law and trend 
of the decision of the Apex Court and I am not in 
clined to give them any other relief except the relief 
of regularisation jul Cult . I in the manner as statçd 
below .

AWARD 
The Central Government in the Ministry of Labour has, 
in exercise of the powers conferred by clause (d ) of sub 
section ( 1 ) and sub - section ( 2 - A ) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, referred the above -mentioned 
disputes for adjudication to this Tribunal. 


7 . I find that the management had appeared and filed 
written statement-cuoi-, ejoinder stating, inter - alia , that he 
referenco were not maintainable and demand of the work 
men after their retrenchment by the concerned contractor , 
Com India Ltd . was without any basis and their claim for 
rgularisation against non - existence job . It is also stated 
that sinking work is not regular nature of job and it is 
required nfter very long gap in the life of a coal mine. It 
is also stated that work of the concerned workmen was super 
vised by the Mining Engineer appointed by the Contractor , 
Cem India Ltd and the concerned workmen were engaged 
for temporary nature of job of loading and unloading of 
stone, carth materials etc . and their , sectioin and jippoint 
ment was made by the Contractor and after completion of 
work the contractor paid them retirement benefit and their 
wages were also paid by the said contractor and the manage 
ment has nothing to do with the same. It is further said 
that the concemed workmen were recruited by the contractor 
company and were paid their wages , bonus, gratuity and 
Retirement benefits by the contractor company and they 
were also allotted works and duties by the officers and 
staff of the contractor company. Therefore if any claim is 
made at all of the concerned workmen is duc it is against 
the contractor company , Cem India Ltd , and not with the 
present management. It is also said that the job of shaft 
sinking is not availablo in the Colliery nor it is expected 
to bo available in the next 20 years and it is finally said . 
that the demand of the workmen was not justilled and they 
aro not entitled for any relief, 


8 . By way of rejoinder to the written statement of the 
workmen the same has been denied specifically para wise 
said to be not fully correct and against the facts of the 
caso . Statements made in para 5 , 8 and 9 to 11 of tho 
written statement are said to be incorrect, 


2 . Hearing of all the three above -noted references have
